=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integer sequences A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) and B=(B_1,B_2,\ldots,B_N) of length N, and integer sequences X=(X_1,X_2,\ldots,X_K) and Y=(Y_1,Y_2,\ldots,Y_K) of length K.
For each k=1,2,\ldots,K, find \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^{X_k} \sum_{j=1}^{Y_k} |A_i-B_j|.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N
B_1 B_2 \ldots B_N
K
X_1 Y_1
X_2 Y_2
\vdots
X_K Y_K

Output

Print K lines.
The i-th line (1\le i\le K) should contain the answer for k=i.

Constraints


- 1\le N\le 10^5
- 0\le A_i,B_j\le 2\times 10^8
- 1\le K\le 10^4
- 1\le X_k,Y_k\le N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2
2 4
3 5
4
1 1
1 2
2 1
2 2

Sample Output 1

1
4
2
6

For k=1, the answer is |A_1-B_1|=1, so print 1 on the first line.
For k=2, the answer is |A_1-B_1|+|A_1-B_2|=1+3=4, so print 4 on the second line.
For k=3, the answer is |A_1-B_1|+|A_2-B_1|=1+1=2, so print 2 on the third line.
For k=4, the answer is |A_1-B_1|+|A_1-B_2|+|A_2-B_1|+|A_2-B_2|=1+3+1+1=6, so print 6 on the fourth line.

Sample Input 2

5
1163686 28892 1263085 2347878 520306
1332157 1202905 2437161 1291976 563395
5
5 3
1 5
2 3
1 2
5 5

Sample Output 2

13331322
2209746
6366712
207690
20241215

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

g = b*pos - pref[pos] + (pref[S]-pref[pos]) - b*(S-pos)

We have static B sequence and dynamic small A set. Query cross-sum between A_set (size ≤S) and B_prefix (size Y). There is a known method using wavelet tree: You can compute sum of absolute differences between a set of values A_set and a range of B in O(|A_set| log σ) by querying each A. There is a "range tree" where you traverse value domain recursively: For a node covering value range [lo,hi], count A in left, B in left, etc. Cross absolute sum can be computed recursively:
cross_sum(A_set, B_prefix) = sum over pairs |a-b|.
If we have wavelet tree over B prefix and A_set values, we can compute by recursion:
At node with value range [l,r], let mid. Split A into A_left,A_right; B into B_left,B_right.
Pairs within same side recurse. Pairs across sides: if a in left, b in right, then |a-b| = b-a; if a in right, b in left, |a-b|=a-b. Need sums of values in each side. If we can get counts and sums of A_set and B_prefix in value ranges quickly, we can compute recursively. Number of nodes visited maybe O(log σ) if we aggregate? Let's derive.

Cross_sum = Σ_{a∈A} ( a*cntB_≤a? no). Recursive formula:
Let A_L,A_R, B_L,B_R by value ≤mid/>mid.
Cross = cross(A_L,B_L)+cross(A_R,B_R) + cross_AC(A_L,B_R)+cross_AC(A_R,B_L).
For cross_AC(A_L,B_R): all a≤mid < b, so sum = |A_L|*sumB_R - sumA_L*|B_R|.
Similarly A_R,B_L: sum = sumA_R*|B_L| - |A_R|*sumB_L.
If we recurse until ranges small, number of nodes could be O(number of distinct values in A * log σ) but maybe can prune if one side empty. Since A_set size S, worst O(S log σ). But maybe using wavelet tree and A_set sorted, can do O(S log σ) anyway.

Could use a Fenwick tree where each node stores count and sum as tuple? Still two arrays, prefix sum loops can update both in same while loop, so per pair one loop over indices, inside access two arrays. That's ~18 iterations, not 36, if we compute both prefix sums in same loop. Yes! We can write:
def prefix(idx):
    c=s=0
    while idx>0:
        c += bitc[idx]; s += bits[idx]; idx -= idx & -idx
    return c,s

  Using bisect_left(vals, b) pos. Since vals sorted.

   a = A[i]
   r = bisect_right(sorted_unique_B, a)  # number of unique B values ≤ a, 1..M
   cnt_le, sum_le = bit_prefix(r)

Need confirm formula with 0-index Y count. For B prefix length y (1..N):
cnt_le = #B_j≤a.
sum_le = sum of those.
sum_gt = total_sum - sum_le.
cnt_gt = y - cnt_le.
Σ|a-B| = Σ_{B≤a}(a-B) + Σ_{B>a}(B-a)
= a*cnt_le - sum_le + sum_gt - a*cnt_gt
= a*cnt_le - sum_le + (total_sum - sum_le) - a*(y - cnt_le)
= total_sum + a*(2*cnt_le - y) - 2*sum_le. Correct.

BIT coordinate compression:
- `unique_B = sorted(set(B))`, M=len(unique_B).
- For each B_j, rank = bisect_left(unique_B, B_j)+1 (1-index).
- To query ≤a, r = bisect_right(unique_B, a). This returns number of unique values ≤a, which equals max rank. BIT prefix(r).
- Update BIT at rank of B_j with count 1 and sum B_j.

BIT implementation:
bitc = [0]*(M+1)
bits = [0]*(M+1)
def add(idx, val):
    while idx <= M:
        bitc[idx] += 1
        bits[idx] += val
        idx += idx & -idx
def sum_prefix(idx):
    c=s=0
    while idx>0:
        c += bitc[idx]
        s += bits[idx]
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return c,s

    while current_y < y:
        b = B[current_y]

        add(idx, b)
        current_y += 1
    total_sum = prefB[y]
    start = (X[k]//S)*S
    # iterate i from start to X[k]-1
    for i in range(start, X[k]):
        a = A[i]
        r = bisect_right(unique_B, a)
        cnt_le, sum_le = prefix(r)
        tail_ans[k] += prefB[y] + a*(2*cnt_le - y) - 2*sum_le

def add_block(p):
    vals = A[p*S:(p+1)*S]
    vals.sort()
    pref = [0]*(S+1)
    # compute pref
    acc=0
    for i,v in enumerate(vals):
        acc += v; pref[i+1]=acc
    running=0
    base_local=base; B_local=B; bl=bisect_left; m=S; pref_local=pref
    for j,b in enumerate(B_local):
        pos = bl(vals, b)
        ls = pref_local[pos]
        rs = pref_local[m] - ls
        running += b*pos - ls + rs - b*(m-pos)
        base_local[j+1] += running
This creates pref list each block. Could compute prefix sums in a list. Fine.

Implementation details for `add_block`:
```
def add_block(p, base, A, B, S, bisect_left):
    vals = A[p*S:(p+1)*S]
    vals.sort()
    pref = [0]*(S+1)
    s=0
    for i,v in enumerate(vals):
        s += v
        pref[i+1] = s
    running = 0
    m = S
    base_local = base
    B_local = B
    pref_local = pref
    vals_local = vals
    bl = bisect_left
    for j, b in enumerate(B_local):
        pos = bl(vals_local, b)
        ls = pref_local[pos]
        rs = pref_local[m] - ls
        running += b * pos - ls + rs - b * (m - pos)
        base_local[j+1] += running
```
Could compute `b * (m - pos)` and `b*pos`; maybe `b*m - b*pos`? Use `b * (m - pos)`.
`running += b * pos - ls + rs - b * (m - pos)` = `running += b * (2*pos - m) + rs - 2*ls`? Let's derive:
b*pos - ls + rs - b*(m-pos) = b*(2*pos - m) + rs - ls? Wait rs = total - ls. So = b*(2pos - m) + total - 2ls. Since total=pref[m]. This uses total once. Let's compute: b*pos - ls + (total-ls) - b*(m-pos) = b*(2pos - m) + total - 2ls. This is fewer operations. Use:
total = pref_local[m]
g = b * (2*pos - m) + total - 2*ls
running += g
Check sample: vals [2,4], total6, b=3,pos1,m2 =>3*(0)+6-4=2. Good. b=5,pos2=>5*(2)+6-12=4. Good.
So add_block:
```
total_val = pref[m]
for j,b in enumerate(B):
    pos=bl(vals,b)
    ls=pref[pos]
    running += b*(2*pos - m) + total_val - 2*ls
    base[j+1] += running
```
This is faster.

Tail BIT formula:
`total + a*(2*cnt - y) - 2*sum_le`. Similar.

def bit_prefix(idx):
    c=s=0
    while idx:
        c += bitc[idx]
        s += bits[idx]
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return c,s
```

B_rank loop: j0 r0 g2 running2 base[1]=2; j1 r1 g4 running6 base[2]=6. Good.

Now, if B value less than all vals: vals [2,4], b=1: ptr0, ls0, g=1*(-2)+6=4? Sum |2-1|+|4-1|=1+3=4. Good.
If b between vals: b=3 g2. If b greater all: b=5 g4. Good.

Now, tail BIT formula with duplicate B: Suppose B=[3,3], unique_B[3], B_rank0[0,0], A=2,Y=2. BIT count=2,sum=6,total=6,y=2,a=2,r=0 => T=6+2*(0-2)=2. Brute |2-3|+|2-3|=2. Good.

Now, let's think about if A_rank_count uses bisect_right(unique_B,a). For a=3, unique_B[3], r=1. BIT prefix(1) count=2,sum=6,total=6,y=2 => T=6+3*(4-2)-12=0. Brute |3-3|+|3-3|=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if B prefix total `prefB[y]` is precomputed. Good.

Now, let's consider if final answer can be negative due to formula? No.

Now, let's think about if there is an even simpler formula using max and precompute block pairs? Our approach okay.

Now, let's ensure code uses `sys.setrecursionlimit` not needed.

Now, let's write final code in our mind:

```
import sys, math, bisect

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx = 0
    N = data[idx]; idx += 1
    A = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
    B = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
    K = data[idx]; idx += 1
    Xs = [0]*K
    Ys = [0]*K
    for i in range(K):
        Xs[i] = data[idx]; Ys[i] = data[idx+1]; idx += 2

    unique_B = sorted(set(B))
    M = len(unique_B)
    B_rank0 = [bisect.bisect_left(unique_B, b) for b in B]
    A_rank = [bisect.bisect_right(unique_B, a) for a in A]

    prefB = [0]*(N+1)
    s=0
    for i,b in enumerate(B):
        s += b
        prefB[i+1] = s

    L = max(1, M.bit_length())
    if K * L == 0: # not possible
       S=1
    else:
       S = int(2.0 * N / math.sqrt(K * L))
    if S < 1: S = 1
    if S > N: S = N

    full_ans = [0]*K
    base = [0]*(N+1)
    pref = [0]*(S+1)
    g_by_val = [0]*M

    def add_block(p):
        vals = A[p*S:(p+1)*S]
        vals.sort()
        acc = 0
        # pref local
        pref_loc = pref
        for i, v in enumerate(vals):
            acc += v
            pref_loc[i+1] = acc
        m = S
        total = pref_loc[m]
        ptr = 0
        g = g_by_val
        uniq = unique_B
        # compute g for each unique B value
        for bi, b in enumerate(uniq):
            while ptr < m and vals[ptr] < b:
                ptr += 1
            ls = pref_loc[ptr]
            g[bi] = b * (2 * ptr - m) + total - 2 * ls
        running = 0
        base_loc = base
        br = B_rank0
        for j, r in enumerate(br):
            running += g[r]
            base_loc[j+1] += running

    order_x = sorted(range(K), key=Xs.__getitem__)
    added = 0
    for k in order_x:
        x = Xs[k]
        target = x // S
        while added < target:
            add_block(added)
            added += 1
        full_ans[k] = base[Ys[k]]

    tail_ans = [0]*K
    total_tail = 0
    for x in Xs:
        total_tail += x % S
    if total_tail:
        bitc = [0]*(M+1)
        bits = [0]*(M+1)
        order_y = sorted(range(K), key=Ys.__getitem__)
        cur_y = 0
        # local variables for speed
        bitc_loc = bitc; bits_loc = bits; M_loc = M
        B_loc = B; B_rank_loc = B_rank0
        A_loc = A; A_rank_loc = A_rank
        prefB_loc = prefB
        tail_ans_loc = tail_ans
        Xs_loc = Xs; Ys_loc = Ys; S_loc = S
        for k in order_y:
            y = Ys_loc[k]
            while cur_y < y:
                b = B_loc[cur_y]
                bi = B_rank_loc[cur_y] + 1
                while bi <= M_loc:
                    bitc_loc[bi] += 1
                    bits_loc[bi] += b
                    bi += bi & -bi
                cur_y += 1
            total = prefB_loc[y]
            start = (Xs_loc[k] // S_loc) * S_loc
            x = Xs_loc[k]
            ans = tail_ans_loc[k]
            for i in range(start, x):
                r = A_rank_loc[i]
                c = 0
                ssum = 0
                j = r
                while j:
                    c += bitc_loc[j]
                    ssum += bits_loc[j]
                    j -= j & -j
                ans += total + A_loc[i] * (2 * c - y) - 2 * ssum
            tail_ans_loc[k] = ans

    out = '\n'.join(str(full_ans[i] + tail_ans[i]) for i in range(K))
    sys.stdout.write(out)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's double-check the mathematical decomposition with full/tail:
For query (X,Y), let bx = X//S, rx = X%S.
Full blocks p=0..bx-1. Tail indices i=bx*S .. X-1.
Full_ans = Σ_{p<bx} E_p(Y) where E_p(Y)=Σ_{i in block p}Σ_{j≤Y}|A_i-B_j|.
Tail_ans = Σ_{i in tail}Σ_{j≤Y}|A_i-B_j|.
Sum = all i≤X. Correct.
